ParlaMint-SI_2019-05-21-SDZ8-Redna-08.ana.u82	Thank you for your word. Good day to you all! I can come before the public today, and I can say that this law will not affect even the pension raise, but, above all, the politicisation of the remaining state property. We were able to read days ago that in Slovenia 1 out of 4 people are over 65 years old and that in 2020 there will be more people over 65 years of age than children up to 5 years old. The article also presents examples of how important it is that countries are aware of the importance of ageing societies and begin to prepare measures in this area. One measure is the reactivation of pensions and pensioners. Throughout the world and Europe, they are increasingly aware of the importance of the knowledge and experience of the retired generation for the development of the country on the one hand and of the importance of the work of pensioners, if they so wish, for their social inclusion, health and material situation. However, we have had debates on the challenges of ageing societies for decades that do not yet have the right conclusions. Sometimes a person gets the feeling that some experts who have been working within the state administration for ten or 20 years have been studying, warning, warning, but never giving any solutions in this area, even though there is almost no citizen who would not know that if we don't make the right moves soon, we will strike in two decades as lead zeppelin. Some things are moving, though. The minimum pension was fixed in 2017. The long-term care law is being prepared, various pilot projects are being carried out in the field of the elderly, and the annual allowance is higher. The Economic and Social Council is holding a debate on the gradual extension of the retirement age from 65 to 67 years to 2034, a gradual rate of calculation of pensions will also be raised, pensioners will be able to work and under certain conditions to receive a full pension. For many years there have also been discussions on the establishment of a demographic fund aimed at ensuring long-term stability of the pension system. The legislative framework that serves as the basis for setting up the Fund goes back years. The first legal basis for the establishment of the demographic fund was adopted in 2012, but has unfortunately not been established by any government since then. The main differences have always touched on two fundamental issues: what assets the demographic fund should acquire and what the way of managing this property should be. So whether the fund should receive portfolio or strategic investments or both and how the Fund will manage itself, whether it will play a decisive role in its policy management through the National Assembly or not. In the letter of Marjan Šarca, as opposed to the SDS proposal that we are considering today, we are advocating the separation of state investments into the strategic ones managed by the Slovenian State Holding and the portfolios managed by the demographic fund. In addition, we believe that the National Assembly should not be a assembly of the demographic fund and that the fund supervisors cannot be determined according to the electoral result or the number of members of the individual party. We are of the opinion that supervisors must be experts, also proposed by civil society in addition to the government. These are also two main reasons why we cannot confirm the SDS proposal. But since we are aware that we need a demographic fund, the Ministry of Finance has already started drafting a law on the establishment of a demographic fund, which the coalition has already held quite a few discussions. In the List of Marjan Šarc, we believe that the government law will be harmonised and we will adopt it in the National Assembly this year. The establishment of the Fund would also address the high level of public investment managed by different operators and thus increase governance transparency. Why are we in the LMŠ, unlike the SDS, defending the division of state property into two pillars: portfolio and strategic? Portfolio investments are more suitable for the demographic fund, according to our assessment, as they enable the pursuit of exclusive returns and do not follow the broader strategic objectives. However, strategic investments need to be made aware that they do not merely pursue financial objectives, we also pursue wider social interest and public access to services under equal conditions. This is precisely why the country does not sell strategic investments. Let me illustrate. Without electricity, it's hard to imagine life. It would be irresponsible for Slovenian energy to be privatised, therefore, to be sold. It is the same with water resources and water supply, state forests, and Luka Koper, as well as with railway or road infrastructure and the Telekom communication network. This is not the case with portfolio investments. The principle of dispersion is followed in order to make it easier to maximize the yield. This is the essence of establishing a demographic fund or co-financing a pension fund. And there's something else. Ownership of strategic investments means ownership in good and bad. And in a bad time, this can quickly become a burden. Why? In the event that we have pooled strategic and portfolio investments in one fund, this means that when a strategic firm is in difficulty and cannot be sold, it is resolved or recapitalised by means of the fund, i.e. also by means of which we are nominating pensions. Why do we not support the SDS proposal in LMŠ to manage state assets? Because this would mean a return a few decades ago to the politicisation of public investment management. This principle is nothing less for us in the LMŠ than the rest of some old times, the times of related services, the times of domestic capitalism, which ultimately is just under the cover of national interest – as in the case of tied trade in the sale of the Lavavarna Laško – has led to a severe erosion of state ownership and perhaps, as a result, to the loss of sovereignty in certain areas. The management of state assets, as established a few years ago by the SDH Act and the State Capital Investment Management Strategy, has finally become more professional and withdrawn from everyday policy. Of course, we would be lying if we said it was optimal, but it is significantly better than before SDH was established and the adoption of the Country Capital Investment Management Strategy. Initially, I have pointed out that the elderly will be one of the largest social groups in the near future, if not the largest, which, due to the progress of medicine and lifestyle, will be healthy and willing to be active in professional and social life. For times are changing rapidly. Sometimes people died when they were 65 years old, and today they're quite easily passed by a 20-mile marathon. Therefore, the mere establishment of the demographic fund and the raising of the retirement age limit will not solve the problem in which we can find ourselves in two decades. Productivity will need to be increased, which also means more investment in R & D, which is of course what this government is doing. In addition to increasing productivity, it will also be necessary to start thinking outside the box – out of the box, as it is now popular to call it, in the integration of the elderly into social and professional life and in the acquisition of new resources for pensions. Your Honor! If ever, now is the last time to start a change towards ensuring a safe and active age in the future. The establishment of a demographic fund is not the only and most important step in this direction, but it is, and we are sure of this in the LMŠ, certainly necessary, but not in the way SDS proposes. Thank you.

The ownership and supervisory function would be exercised or controlled by parliamentary political parties through the National Assembly, even contrary to the legal order of the Republic of Slovenia. Let me remind you that some past attempts to establish a demographic fund have already remained at an informal level precisely because of the lack of consistency of solutions similar to those proposed by SDS Members today. The independent and autonomous reserve demographic fund is intended to adapt or respond to the projected demographic changes in the future. Proposers are sure to know the modern concepts of demographic reserve funds, so they might say that we were surprised by what too much of the domestic content solutions in your proposal for the law has been said. The demographic reserve funds are generally assets set up by the State in order to ensure in advance a sufficient amount of funds in the pension fund for periods when, due to negative demographic trends, its expenditure will be higher than its income. States as founders of such funds shall follow the principles of profitability, security, dispersion, dynamics, liquidity and investment independence. In the OECD, there are even international recommendations and principles on the functioning of the Funds, which set out the legal forms, governance structures and investment policies. Such a modern concept of the reserve demographic fund is also always advocated in the Modern Centre Party. We are therefore committed to maintaining the role of the Slovenian State Holding Company, which, in line with the guidelines on good corporate governance, would be managed in a concentrated manner by strategic and important capital investments of the country. In doing so, we believe that Kad should be transformed into a demographic portfolio fund as a self-sustaining owner of his property, as it could only ensure the security and profitability of his assets. At the Modern Centre Party, we regret that the former government has been halfway through the preparation of the Demographic Fund Act. The proposal was prepared and sent to public hearing in June 2017. Then there was no final coordination and confirmation of the government, and the conceptual differences between the coalition parties were too great. We have already noted that the objective of the independent demographic fund is to ensure the long-term stability of the pension system, but this obviously did not coincide with the short-term objectives of some coalition parties, probably due to the upcoming state-level elections. We therefore welcome the intention of the current Minister of Finance in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre and estimate that the concept of the reserve demographic fund, as recently presented to the coalition partners, is in line with what we have already taken up in the Party of the Modern Centre in the previous mandate. In this regard, we note in particular that the concept of the demographic fund must be designed to attract all those stakeholders to whom or to whom the fund is intended in the future. These are not just today’s pensioners, but, above all, today’s youth. However, the establishment of a demographic fund should not be an excuse to avoid making the necessary changes to the pension system as soon as possible. We cannot support the proposal for a law on the Slovenian Demographic Fund for all the reasons listed in the Group of the Modern Centre Party. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-05-21-SDZ8-Redna-08.ana.u88	Thank you very much, Madam President. Dear colleagues, hello! In Levika, we have long advocated the establishment of a demographic fund that would accumulate state assets and then devote income in particular to the financing of pensions. The key question is what kind of demographic fund we want to set up. In Levica, we do not want another fund that will sell state property, but a fund that will actually accumulate state assets and actually acquire new investments. We do not want another fund that will be diverted from democratic control under the cover of autonomy and independence. We also do not want another fund to pursue only and only the objective of profitability in asset management, regardless of potential social or environmental risks. The demographic fund, as proposed by the SDS, appears at first glance to be a brilliant solution to demographic problems and a great gift to all pensioners. However, the demographic fund, which will be allowed to sell state property without major problems, will eventually cause pensioners more damage than benefits. Why? The purchases from the sale of state property represent only a single budget income, yielding from sustained investments in which the state retains its ownership, representing a permanent and long-term source of income. Therefore, the demographic fund should primarily pursue the objective of long-term ownership of key strategic and important investments of the country. In this regard, the proposal for a law is totally inappropriate. The demographic fund, as envisioned by the SDS, will sell state property even easier than the Slovenian state holding company does. SDH investments are divided into strategic ones where the State must maintain a 50% plus one share; then important where the State must maintain a 25% plus one share and portfolios with which SDH freely disposes. Although the proposed Demographic Fund Act sets a threshold of 50% and one share in strategic investments, not a threshold of 25% and one share in significant investments. This degrades the country's significant investments to portfolio level. This means that the demographic fund will also be able to fully sell the country's significant investments. Instead of deleting the security guards from privatisation, new tighter fuses should be established to prevent the sale of state property. The setting of minimum shares for strategic investments is not sufficient. State property should be locked in accordance so that it could not be sold without the consent of a two-thirds majority in Parliament on the basis of a serious analysis. If the demographic fund is allowed to sell state property without serious safeguards, the pensioners will eventually suffer the most. The second problem of the proposal is in the organisation of the Fund. This should follow autonomy and independence, but only individuals proposed by the parliamentary groups would sit on the Supervisory Committee. In doing so, the provisions of the law governing the participation of workers in management would not apply if we agreed that the demographic fund would become a hostage to the interests of the parties every time the government clicks. The disputed practice of political influence can only be prevented by democratisation of the management of the Fund and, contrary to the current proposal, representatives of workers from the Economic and Social Council should be included in the Supervisory Council. And the third problem is, this concerns the objectives of governance. The first priority is to increase the value of investments or to ensure as high a return as possible for owners. Profitability is certainly an important criterion to be taken into account in management, but it is not the only criteria. Higher returns should not be achieved at the expense of labour rights or environmental pollution. Each investment must also take into account the aspect of respect for labour rights and other social and environmental risks. The proposed version of the demographic fund is a pale copy of SDH, which is managed by strategic and important companies as a mere portfolio of shares that can be sold out easily in short-term hunting. We will not support the establishment of such a fund in the Left. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-05-21-SDZ8-Redna-08.ana.u89	Thank you very much. The next position will be presented by the mag. Mr Matthew Tonin on behalf of the NSI Group of Members. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-05-21-SDZ8-Redna-08.ana.u90	Dear Members and Members, good afternoon! Finally, we are discussing the real subject, a topic that will burden us in the years to come, so it is essential that we begin to speak responsibly and seriously today. Slovenia is certainly an ageing society. Current data shows that we are the ninth oldest country in the world. If demographic trends do not change significantly in the coming years, Slovenia will become the fifth oldest country in the world in 2050, with certain consonants. Pressure will increase on our pension system, on our health system, and if we want to adapt to these trends today, then measures are needed today, not tomorrow. Women in Slovenia on average are 81 years old, on the other hand men are slightly less than 74 years old. However, it is encouraging to note that on average men live on average for more than 10 years than Slovenia's independence. Our current pension system is a classic flow system, which means that pension money is spent across the street. This money that we are paying for our pension today and for our salaries, it's the same month's pension for the existing pensioner. Sometimes the Members walk in the field and we get knocked by the sleeve and ask us: “Where is my money, which I have paid for 40 years in my salary, for my pension?” The truth must then be told that this money is not, of course, because this money was not loaded anywhere, but it was spent on pensions every month. Therefore, in a pension system such as Slovenia has, the relationship between pensioners and employees is extremely important. In the previous country, or 30 years ago, as long as the relationship between the employee and the pensioner was 1 to 3 — that three employees worked for one pensioner — such a system may have been sustainable. Today this is a ratio of 1 to 1,5 and in times of crisis this ratio fell even to 1.3. If I simplify, for a pensioner who received 600 euros in pension, there were as many as three workers with a minimum wage during the crisis. These are the facts and realities in our pension system. According to the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance, it took place in the previous year, 2018, for all pensions in Slovenia EUR 5.3 billion and EUR 5,000 300 million. Of these 5,000 300 million euros, 4 000 244 million euros have been raised from our salaries, which is 80% of what has been given to pensions. So there's a fifth of the money missing. And who contributed this fifth, who contributed this 20% of the money? State. Last year, the country contributed a thousand 56 million euros to the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance. So for our pension system, one-fifth of the money, which is said to be pensions, was contributed by the state. Why did this money have to be contributed? It's very simple because our salaries haven't collected enough money. That the current system is unrestrained and needs to be adapted, it is clear, and will be more unsustainable every year because the pensioners will be more and more. If we say over the thumb that the country is already giving a thousand million euros or 1 billion euros a year, every year, I stress that this represents a tenth of the budget for the pension system. 10% of the budget is to cover every year what is missing in the pension register. That is to say and write, about one track each year would have been easy to build if there was no mannequin in the pension box. It is a matter of concern that, despite the fact that at a time of high economic growth, the state still allocates 20% of the pension fund, a fifth of the state draws the pension fund at the time of the largest economic growth, when employment is extremely high, when it has also paid a lot into the pension fund. If you rotate this picture slightly back to 2013, you will find that the country's contribution to the pension fund was as much as a third or 30%. What are the solutions to make our pension system more sustainable? I can tell you that there are three solutions. First of all, we have more employees who are paying into the pension fund. In any event, every employee is a dual benefit for the country because it pays into pension systems, into health systems, and on the other hand, it is not receiving different social assistance. So with more employees paying for their contributions, more rechargeable pension bills would be available. But of course it is easy to say this and much harder to do it because it does not depend on any administrative action, but it depends on the economy, on its agility, on its functioning. The more successful the economy, the more people are employed and the more he has paid into the pension register. This is one pretty difficult solution. The second, rather unpopular, is that we should work longer. We live longer and therefore there are proposals that retirement age should also be increased. If today those working for 35 or more years simply mention that retirement age should be raised, they are very quickly angry or in bad will. If I speak from home experience, when one parent has just retired and the other is just before retirement, then I can say that whenever there is a pension reform on the agenda at Sunday lunchtime or a possible rise in retirement age, we come very quickly, that there is only a picture and the sound is gone. That is why it is very unpopular to talk about such solutions in politics. And then we come up with a third solution, which is that from the existing money that the state has at its disposal, we try to create as much as possible to cover that fifth that every year needs a pension or pension system, a pension fund. So the fundamental question is how to create more from existing assets. First, about the demographic fund. The demographic fund sounds very nice, we've talked about it many times. For example, Desus has repeatedly taken the initiative to establish a demographic fund before the elections. According to the household, the demographic fund is supposed to represent a manager who is supposed to nominate state property in order to thus cover a hole in the pension system. But if we look very closely at Desus's proposal, which says, let's form a demographic fund and let this fund be formed so that the current Kad is transformed into a demographic fund. This Kad now says and writes every year that the pension fund is 50 million euros. 50 million euros. While only students contribute €60 million each year through student travel. That's more than Kad. If I go back to the beginning that, at the time of big economic growth, the pension fund has run out of thousands of euros, one billion, one thousand million euros – what will we do with 50 million euros? Every year there are thousands of millions of euros running out, and Desus says they would transform Kad into a demographic fund that is now paying 50 million euros. With 50 million euros, there is certainly no way to solve the current problems of the pension system, and in some ways it is misleading to say that with the establishment of the demographic fund, all problems will be solved, but not to say that the revenues from this title would perhaps be EUR 50 million, which is less than the students' contribution today. Also those famous allegations that we have ensured that 10% of the sale of state property to cover losses in the pension system and from this title has so far collected some more than EUR 100 million. Again, what helps us 100 million euros, and if we miss 1,000 million euros every year! So our solutions must be a little more ambitious, a little more daring to cover this loss made by the pensioner's treasury. If we assume that the country had €10 billion in assets in 2017, then it is now a fundamental question of how to create as much of these €10 billion as possible to cover the billions of Euros of losses made by the pension system each year. If we sell all our assets at once and we are happy about how it goes with the purchases of the property to cover the losses of the pension system, this joy will soon be over. When we sell all the assets, then it will simply not be able to put anything into the pension register anymore or if I make it very simple and very caricatured, I would say that if we sell everything we currently have, and that we can endure an incredibly large, 10 billion euros, such as some estimates of Slovenian state property, it would be sufficient in the current trends there for the bad 10 years of coverage of the collapse of our pension system. And then what? Then we're back to square one. Then we're in trouble again. Therefore, today we need to think about how to create as much of what we have as possible for our citizens. If we look at examples in the north, for example, if you look at Norway, you can find that they put all the revenues they generate from oil into one single fund and then invest this fund around the world to raise more funds to make money for hard times, to have money for pensions, for possible losses of the pension fund. That is why this debate today is certainly a step in the right direction to create as much of our assets as possible for our people. If you listen to the Left, they will say that we absolutely must not privatize and sell anything. But the state has proven to be a bad operator so far, and the result was that these companies have gone backwards, put fewer dividends in the state budget, and ultimately, because of poor governance, it can also mean that they are failing in the end. So it's not all about being opposed to privatisation. Privatisation can be one way for a country to sell a company that itself does not know how to do well, and this makes a smart investment in the purchase that it does not spend it all at once, but to fertilize it and that it eventually gets more out of it. At least that's how it's supposed to be in an ideal situation. The law, which is before us today, as I said, can be a step in the right direction to open up the right themes to open up the topics that this Parliament must talk about in the long run. Of course, I also see one great risk in this law, and this is the management of this Slovenian demographic fund. If we have everything gathered in one place and if this is very well managed, then they can produce extremely large. On the other hand, if there is bad governance and we have everything in one place, it can also mean great losses. In the management of state property, Slovenia has not shown itself in the past, there has been a lot of political recruitment, political bargaining, where the best manager was not selected, but the most elected was our manager. As a result, many wrong decisions are made. Therefore, New Slovenia would support such a solution if we were already going to collect all state property under one roof, so that this property would not be managed by policy, but that, in the example of some other countries, we would prepare an international invitation to tender and get what we can, if possible, the best, manager and also pay it for efficiency. If you Google a little, you can find some articles on the management of state property in Romania. As long as the villa is a state political administration, this state property has created losses all the time. But when they made an international call and invited the best foreign operators to manage this property and set a goal, all of a sudden this state property began to generate interesting and beneficial revenues. So the key to everything is management. The fundamental thing on which we will focus in New Slovenia in the future is how to ensure the effective management of our state assets. I imagine that in this House it will be difficult to gather a serious majority to make one such comprehensive demographic fund, because political differences are very personal, so it may be the best solution for us to change the current pension system and adjust it in the direction, say and write Australia, which would mean that the state and its system would be responsible for providing all pensioners with the same pension, at least at the level of the poverty threshold. If I said that in the amount, let's say today it would be about €620, and from this amount forward, each of its luck is a blacksmith. In the given circumstances and in the given political relationship, this may seem to us to be the most intelligent and even the most possible solution in the long term. As action must be taken today, demography is unrelenting. We are pleased, however, that we have finally begun to open up these topics, which are the subject of the future and which need a solution now.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-05-21-SDZ8-Redna-08.ana.u110	Thank you for your word! There is no doubt that the demographic fund is needed because of negative demographic projections and the ageing population. The idea of a demographic fund is not new, but it was already envisaged by the Pension and Disability Insurance Act at the end of 2012. At that time, it was stipulated that a special law would regulate the operation and financing of the demographic reserve fund within two years. Unfortunately, the previous government did not find a common language and the law was not dealt with in the National Assembly because of the fundamental issue that the demographic fund should receive and what the way of managing this fund should be. The purpose of the demographic fund is to co-finance the ever-increasing expenditure of the pension fund at a time when they will increasingly be burdened by the state budget. Most of the pension funds are collected through contributions, which is currently good at 4 billion, while some of them — about a billion — are contributed by the state treasury from its budget. Since neither the increase in the contribution rates due to the competitiveness of the economy nor the increase in the share of the budget due to fiscal stability are currently possible, the demographic fund is one of the positive solutions. The demographic fund is primarily not intended for higher pensions, but only means lower expenditure of the state budget to pay the difference between the revenue of the PSIZ and its expenditure on pension payments. The transfer of all capital investments owned by the Republic of Slovenia without exception to the demographic fund, as proposed by the SDS in the proposal for a law, is unacceptable because this way does not create a demographic fund, but rather an alternative system for managing the country's capital investments, which is not the purpose of the fund. In addition to profitability, the demographic funds must also take into account the security of investment, liquidity, fragmentation and independence. All these principles must be respected if we want the Fund to serve its purpose. With all investments, portfolios and strategic, under the same roof, we place the fund in a very risky position. Therefore, only portfolio investments in which we can follow the principle of dispersion and the return on investment, but not the strategic ones that we need to manage actively, should be in line. In the event that a strategic firm appears in difficulty, it should be recapitalised from the money of the demographic fund. Therefore, if we want the demographic fund to serve its purpose, it must have a smart investment policy. In addition to the other reasons stated by the Government in its opinion that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration, the issue of asset management is also a matter where, in terms of constitutional or legal order, the National Assembly cannot be the Assembly of the Slovenian Demographic Fund, as proposed by the proposer. Since the Government is already preparing a proposal that will follow the Fund's core mission and will be presented to the National Assembly in a short time, I will not support the bill today. Thank you.

All of this shows that this is a topic where it is very difficult to reconcile different perspectives and interests, so the obviouss of the previous government are unfounded. At that time three governments have been replaced, and the government is already the fourth to save the demographic fund. Today, the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group tabled a proposal for a law on the Slovenian Demographic Fund that would transfer all state assets to this fund. And most of the proceeds of this property would also be earmarked for pensions. At the starting point, they insist that all state assets be transferred to the demographic fund. This legal solution is intended to improve the situation of pensioners and, on the other hand, to relieve employers and employees of pressure that a large proportion of the earnings should be allocated to pensions. They are proposing a national pension fund to be taken over by the National Assembly and managed by a ruling coalition. This would ensure greater control and more transparent business. In doing so, it should be noted that the SDS has taken the proposal for a law tabled by the Desus Group in the previous term as the basis of its proposal and that the SMC has opposed it. The issue of governance and the structure of investments that should be owned by the Fund led to significant differences. For the Modern Centre Party, only a proposal drawn up by the Ministry of Finance in this mandate followed the proposal of the Ministry of Finance from the previous mandate is acceptable. However, the acquisition and management solution proposed by the SDS is not correct and does not guarantee the Fund's long-term stability. Any new call by the National Assembly and any new government could, through its programmes, establish a policy for the functioning of the demographic fund. Independent, autonomous reserve demographic funds, which we also know in other countries, are designed to adapt or respond to the projected demographic changes in the future. They ensure the creation of the necessary social and economic dynamics in a rapidly ageing society. At the same time, they provide a guarantee of funding for periods of high pension needs. The legal regime of this area has already been established in our country by the Pension and Disability Insurance Act (SPIZ), in order to regulate the operation and financing of this fund within two years. The Slovenian State Holding Act regulates this matter. According to the provisions of this law, Kad has to be transformed into a reserve demographic fund, as provided for by STIZ. The State's Capital Investment Management Strategy Decree also provides that long-term and balanced sustainability of pension funds and public finances requires the creation, operation and financing of the demographic reserve fund. Inflows to the pension register declined during the economic growth period and the number of pensioners increased. The objective of the strategy is also to transform Kad into an independent and autonomous demographic reserve fund, as defined by this law governing pension and disability insurance and the Slovenian State Holding Act. In fact, the demographic reserve funds are a series of state-owned assets which are intended for pre-financing of pension funds during the period when their expenditure will be higher than their income. Pension and disability insurance are areas that are becoming increasingly important in the world and in us as well. The crisis of state pension schemes has deepened since the beginning of the 1970s. In Slovenia, pension funds are provided from active insured contributions, which means that our pension system is still largely based on intergenerational solidarity. In addressing this problem, however, we must take into account two main aspects of the constraints affecting pension systems in most Member States of the European Union. These are: the demographic factor associated with the impact of the decline in fertility and ageing population in Europe and the economic factor associated with the impact of the unemployment rate and the rate of economic growth. When the proposal was tabled in the SDS, all parliamentary parties were invited to jointly draft the final text. Most of the parties announced that the proposal would be examined and responded to the call. We did the same in the Modern Centre, but it will not happen. At this time, the coalition has already agreed on the concept of the demographic fund and has decided to submit its proposal. As I care about the older people, our generation, our children, I will personally continue to actively support the early implementation of those legal provisions regarding the establishment of the demographic reserve fund and its operation. We need to put in place a system that will provide all pensioners with long-term and fair means of life worth living. The purpose of the reserve demographic fund is only to defuse the existing pension system financially; however, there must be no excuse for the non-replacement of the pension system in the future. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-05-21-SDZ8-Redna-08.ana.u202	Thank you for the floor, Mr President. Messengers and Members, ladies and gentlemen! At its 4th extraordinary meeting on 23 April this year, the Council of State reviewed the amendment of the Act on Holidays and Works of Anti-Days in the Republic of Slovenia, adopted a request for the National Assembly to decide again on the amendment of the law. In the State Council, we have obtained quotations from the original texts, international treaties and interpretation of the meanings of the individual terms, and therefore we consider that the change in the name of the holiday and the return to connection is not appropriate and gives meaning to the return or integration of Primorska to the homeland of a purely different meaning. Following the end of World War I and the dissolution of the Austro-Hungarian Empire, the inhabitants of this part of Slovenian ethnic territory, because of the secret and shameful London agreement, were not allowed the right to self-determination, but it belonged to Italy as a spoil of war. At the end of World War II, Primorska became part of the Republic of Slovenia in the then Yugoslavia with the Paris International Treaty. The word “joint” is not used anywhere in the text of the contract. The use of the concept of connection or annexion – in European and global history, we know quite a few cases – is needed in cases where a particular territory is joined by a country based on the occupation: Alsace, Germany, France, Israel, the Goran Planet. In the case of the Italian annexation of coastal Istria, Dalmatia and the islands under the London secret agreement of 2015, we cannot and must not apply the concept of connection, but rather the return or merger of Primorska. Returning means giving back something you stole or took from you. The coastline was stolen, connected, then renegotiated by an international contract. At a symposium held in 1997 at the International Conference of Historians on the anniversary of the Paris Peace Treaty, Croatian historian Petar Strčić warned Slovenian historians that they used the wrong concept of connection, which means annexation, which in this case is not applicable because it speaks of return, derogation. The proposer of the law on the renaming of the holiday refers to the use of the term of accession in Slovenian historians, but we do not agree with this. How sensitive and responsible is the description of the holidays, which preserve the memory of events that are vital to most citizens of our country, we witnessed the events in Bazovica this February. The statements of Tajani you all know and know what our reaction was. In the State Council, we believe that the enforcement of the law in such a form can have adverse international consequences. Primorska has always been part of Slovenian ethical territory, with the difference that the inhabitants of this part of the present country, after the end of World War I, were not granted the right to self-determination for the reasons already mentioned, known. The efforts of the Primors to live together with the native nation in the country continued after World War I — the resistance to violent Germanization, Fascism and all the cruelty of the regime to the indigenous population, which reached its peak in the years of World War II, until Italy’s capitalisation, it maintained national consciousness. Coastal patriotism and coastal partisans were liberated. The home fight did not begin with the decision of the Liberation Front at the time of the inclusion of Primorska, but since 1920 with the burning of the national home in Trieste, forward with the TIGRs, with ČEKO, the chaplains of Martini cheddermaks, Terčelj, Drago Bajec, Alojz Bratuž, the base victims and all who kept the Slovenian language, Slovenian song and culture. Coastal patriotism and coastal partisans were liberated. Therefore, to say that Slovenia has joined Primorsko is an unfair and irresponsible act. The proposal to amend the law appeared both times just before the elections, which is not the most appropriate time for such a sensitive topic. Because of the reckless actions and statements of individuals, we have lost too much territory in history. The bill was not proposed by someone, but by the president of the Committee on Foreign Policy, so its political moves have an even greater international weight. We are citizens and not politicians, these are all too many in Slovenia. We are convinced that Primorska deserves a holiday with a name that connects and acknowledges the desire for self-determination of the population and does not share. The Council of State proposes to the National Assembly not to support the law. Today we have received a statement from a great Slovenian patriot, a market writer, a rights fighter who urges you not to support the law and change the name. Thank you.

This was Slovenian territory. These arguments explain why the historical correct term “connection” is. The fact is that today the decision to be taken will be political. We can say that a person’s return is a better word because he defines that word as the return of something he has lost in the past. Also, someone could use the word merger in a way that unites, which has been divided over many years. In short, unless strictly historical facts are taken into account, each of its terminology can be argued in its own way. And if I take the words from the previous point of view, holidays are days that have special meaning for us or are dedicated to a special event. Some holidays are only important to an individual, others are celebrated by all. At times, though, each individual finds the reason for the celebration, and that is the essence of the holiday and of each individual. Perhaps it would be better if the hours we spent discussing the name of this holiday were to focus on putting more national consciousness into our school system or in general into our society and knowing what we are celebrating on a particular holiday.

Nothing can change that fact. The designation of the accession of Primorska is also more appropriate from the point of view of international law than the return and formerly serves as a protector and not an alibi for various political and territorial claims on our western border. After receiving three nominations from the Ministry of Justice, the President of the Republic also obtained the opinion of the Government and the opinion of the Council of Justice on candidates. Both the Government and the Council of Justice consider that all three candidates, both candidates and candidates, fulfil formal conditions and are fit for this function. After examining the candidates and following consultations with the leaders of the parliamentary groups, the President of the Republic decided to propose to the National Assembly for appointment both candidates and candidates, i.e. Mrs Ana Stanič, Prof. Verico Trstenjak and Prof. Juret Vidmar. Ana Stanič is a Slovenian citizen; otherwise, she is an English and Australian lawyer who has already been appointed an arbitrator in several arbitrations. It has more than 24 years of legal experience in the field of international law, European law, international arbitration. For more than 7 years, it has been classified as the leading international law expert in Britain. It has been recognised for more than 5 years as one of the leading experts in international commercial arbitration, investment arbitration and energy. 11 years ago, after research work at the University of Cambridge and the British Institute of International and Comparative Law and after work in some of the world's most recognised law offices, she established her own law firm to facilitate the provision of conditions for her independent position, integrity and professional approach to clients. Lawyers in various parts of the world enjoy a high moral reputation. Prof. Jure Vidmar has been a regular professor and head of the Department of International Public Law at the Faculty of Law of the University of Maastricht in the Netherlands since 2014. He was a doctor at the University of Salzburg in Austria, then at Nottingham Law School in Great Britain. Currently, he is a guest professor at the Faculty of Law of the University of Ljubljana and since this year he is also a judge of the ad hoc European Court of Human Rights. He was a lecturer at the Faculty of Law of Oxford University in Great Britain, where he taught, among other things, European Union law, international public law, legal philosophy. During that time, he spent one year as a guest member of Harvard Law School. As a member of a group of different experts, he has participated in some of the current international legal issues. When considering the position of Catalonia, on brexit, on the Scottish referendum on independence. Since 2012 he has held a series of lectures every year at the Faculty of Law of the University of Pretoria, South Africa. Prof. Verica Trstenjak, Doctor of Legal Sciences, Professor of European Law at the Faculty of Law of the University of Vienna, former Advocate General at the Court of Justice of the European Union, former Judge at the General Court of the European Union. As Advocate General, European law has been importantly co-created in various areas. From 2012 onwards, he is an external scientific member of the Max Planck Institute in Luxembourg, a member of the management bodies of the European Union Agency for Fundamental Rights in Vienna. He teaches European law at the Faculty of Law in Maribor, at the European Faculty of Law in Ljubljana and at Sigmund Freud University in Vienna. She has performed many specialties and has taught as a guest lecturer at many European, American, Canadian and Australian universities. She is a carrier of subjects in master's studies at universities in Luxembourg, Austria, Canada and Slovenia. The focus of its research work is European Union law in various fields. The President of the Republic considers both candidates and candidates to be highly qualified for the proposed function to be recognised international legal experts with extensive professional bibliography who, by their reputation and work so far, justify their confidence in the functions performed by the member or member of the Permanent Court of Arbitration in The Hague. Thank you very much.
